2

全球半導體設備市場規模快速成長  
根 據 市 調 機 構 數 據 顯 示 ， 全 球 前 段 製 程 設 備 （ Waf er Fa b Equipmen t, 
WFE ）， 2025 年 銷 售 額 創 下 1,169 億 美 元 新 高 ， 預 估 2026 年將再成長
23.1% ，達 1,439 億 美 元 。 此 數 字 較 SEMI 先 前 於 2025 年 底 所 公 布 的 預 測
大 幅 上 修 ， 主 要 受 惠 於 先 進 記 憶 體 、 尤 其 是 HBM 相關 DRAM 技 術 ， 以 及
先 進 邏 輯 應 用 投 資 持 續 增 加 。 隨 著 製 造 商 因 應 AI 應 用 需 求 ， 加 速 擴 充 產 能
及 推 進 技 術 升 級，預 估 2027 年及 2028 年 將 分 別 成 長 21.8% 及 14.1% ，2028
年 市 場 規 模 可 望 突 破 2,000 億 美 元 。  
圖 二 、 全 球 半 導 體 設 備 市 場 預 測

資 料 來 源 ： SEMI 、 康 和 投 顧 整 理 ， 2026/10

工業應用為 最 強 成 長 動 能  
1~3Q26 營 收 占 比 37% ， YOY+38.9% ， 主 要 受 惠 於 全 球 半 導 體 設 備 需 求 擴
張 及 智 能 製 造 自 動 化 升 級 潮 。 內 部 結 構 佔 比 包括 倉 儲 物 流 自 動 化 近 40% 、
半 導 體 設 備 佔 30 ~35% 、 運 輸 / 建設 設 備 線 束 佔 10% 、 能 源 管 理 類 佔 1 0% 。
動能 主 要 來 自 半 導 體 設 備 機 內 / 機 外 訊 號 與 電 源 線 束 組 裝 以 及 倉 儲 物 流 自
動 化 需 求 增 加 ， 27F 營收 YOY+20% 。  
a. 半 導 體 設 備 : 為 最 大 亮 點 ， 從 傳 統 線 束 升 級 至 整 機 機 櫃 組 裝 與 系 統 整 合 。
提 供 機 內 ／ 機 外 訊 號 線 與 電 源 線，並 開 始 整 合 其 他 零 組 件 做 成 小 型 機 櫃
／ 模 組 給 客 戶 。  
✓ 供 應 鏈 地 位 ： 公司 為 全 球 半 導 體 前 段 與 後 段 設 備 大 廠 （ 如  AS ML 、應
用 材 料 等 ） 的 重 要 高 度 客 製 化 線 束 及 系 統 整 合 供 應 商 。  
✓ 應 用 領 域 ： 先 進 節 點 製 造 中 的 光 刻 （ 微 影 ） 、 蝕 刻 和 沉 積 設 備 、 精密
製 程 設 備 中 的 線 束 系 統 、 機 櫃 半 成 品 組 裝 。  
✓ 訂 單 與 產 能 狀 況： 受 產 品 銷 量 增 長 、 複 雜 性 提 升 及 新 客 戶 訂 單 需 求 驅
動，公 司 目 前 通 過 加 班 和 增 加 第 二 班 次 滿 足 產 能 需 求 ；為 進 一 步 擴
充 產 能 ， 斥 資 逾 50 億 元 在 苗 栗 銅 鑼 科 學 園 區 廠 房 擴 建 產 能 ， 主 要
目 的 即 是 用 於 擴 充 半 導 體 設 備 高 階 模 組 與 線 束 生 產 量 能 。 計 劃 於
2Q27 年 正 式 投 入 運 營 ， 預計 增 長 趨 勢 將 持 續 到 2030 年 。

3

✓ 液 冷 散 熱 技 術 延 伸 : 已 展 示 四 款 數 據 中 心 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 原 型（ 兩 款 浸
沒 式 液 冷 、 兩 款 傳 統 液 冷 ） ， 可為 OD M 和 CSP 提 供 客 製 化 熱 管
理 服 務 ， 產品預計 2026 年 下 半 年 開 始 送 樣 ， 2H27 年 對 盈 利 產 生
實 質 性 貢 獻 。  
b. 人 形 機 器 人 ： 機 器 人 線 束 預 估 4Q26 起進 入 量 產 ， 已 取 得 5 家美系 Tier 1
獨 家 訂 單 ， 提 供 整 組 線 束 模 組 （ 獨 家 供 應 ） ， 終 端 應 用 多 在 工 廠 或 倉 儲
物 流 。 預 估 營 收 占 比 2026 年約 1% 、 2027 年約 2% 、 2028 年 有 望 達
13% 。 毛 利 率 預 估 約 40% ， 2028 年 有 機 會 達 10 萬 套 以 上 。  
✓ 產品 : 提 供 完 整 線 纜 模 組 （ 感 測 器 、 連 接 器 、 訊 號 傳 輸 線 、 充 電 線 、 關
節 線 束 等 ） ，涵 蓋 全 身 應 用，單 台 機 器 人 線 束 產 值 約 1,000 – 1,500
美 元 （ 視 整 合 程 度 ， 一 台 可 能 需 要 50 – 150 條 線 束 ） 。  
✓ 技 術 門 檻 高：關 節 線 束 需 通 過 極 高 次 數（ 數 十 萬 至 百 萬 次 ）彎 折 測 試 ，
具 備 耐 彎、耐 壓、防 水、輕 量 化 等 特 性，屬 客 製 化 總 解 決 方 案 （Tota l 
Solution ） ， 多 數 專 案 為 獨 家 供 應 （ Only S our ce ） 。  
✓ 客 戶 ： 已 取 得 約 5 家美系 Tier1 （ 包 括 Figure 等 ） 獨 家 線 束 訂 單 ， 部
分 涵 蓋 中 國 客 戶 （ 以 零 組 件 為 主 ） 。 深 耕 機 器 人 領 域 逾 10 年，從
AGV 、 機 械 手 臂 、 機 器 狗 累 積 經 驗 。  
✓ 策 略 聯 盟 ： 與 宇 隆（ 2233 ）合 作 ， 結 合 線 束 / 控 制 模 組 與 減 速 機（ RV 、
諧 波 、 行 星 ） ， 朝 系 統 級 解 決 方 案 發 展 。  
表 一 、 其他 機 器 人 類 型

資 料 來 源 ： 康 和 投 顧 整 理 ， 2026/10

c. 無 人 機 : 主要 提 供 無 人 機 機 內 線 及 電 池 換 電 櫃 櫃 體 ， 主 要 供 應 2 家 美 系 客
戶。 3Q26 起 開 始 出 貨 ， 26 年 營 收 約 5~6 億元 ， 27 年 估 成 長 至 8~10
億 元 ， 毛 利 率 約 有 30~40% ， 預 計 於 2028 年 放 量 。  
✓ 定位 : 採 取 差 異 化 策 略 ， 聚 焦 高 法 規 門 檻 與 營 運 壁 壘 的 「 商 用 運 輸 」 市
場 （ 物 流 配 送 等 ） ， 避 開 競 爭 激 烈 的 消 費 型 空 拍 應 用 。  
✓ 產品 : 機 內 線 束（ 充 電 線 、 訊 號 傳 輸 線 等 ）、 換 電 櫃（ 電 池 交 換 櫃 體 ），
支 援 快 速 電 池 更 換 。  
✓ 客 戶 ： 以 美 系 為 主 ， 包 括 Ama zon 等 物 流 無 人 機 相 關 業 者 （ 提 供 整 組
解 決 方 案 ） ， 目 前 約 服 務 2 家 美 國 主 要 客 戶 。  
✓ 應用 : 倉 儲 到 配 送 站 點 的 物 流 運 輸 、 最 後 一 哩 路 精 準 配 送 、 與 倉 儲 機 器

人 、 智 慧 安 全 背 心 形 成 完 整 智 慧 物 流 生 態 系 。  
表 二 、 智 慧 倉 儲 無 人 化 生 態 系 統

資 料 來 源 ： 康 和 投 顧 整 理 ， 2026/10

醫 療 及 車 用 產 品 穩 定 成 長  
醫療 ： 1~3Q26 營 收 佔 比 9.8% ， YOY+28.4% ， 以 病 人 監 護 與 影 像 診 斷 設 備
內 部 之 傳 輸 線 束 為 主 ( 中系 ) ， GPM> 平 均 ， 27F 營收 YOY+10~15% 。  
a. 產品 : 專 注 於 高 階 大 型 醫 療 設 備 線 束 ， 包 括  CT （ 電 腦 斷 層 ） 、 MRI （ 核 磁
共 振 ） 、 超 音 波 診 斷 設 備  及  加 護 病 房 （ ICU ） 病 人 監 護 系 統 。  
b. 技 術 壁 壘 ： 醫 療 線 束 要 求 極 高 的 生 物 相 容 性 、 抗 干 擾 性 （ EMI ） 與 長 期 高
穩 定 度 ， 認 證 時 間 長 達 2~3 年 以 上 ， 屬 於 「 高 客 製 化 、 高 利 基 」 市 場 。 
c. 成 長 動 能 : 受 惠 中 國 客 戶 加 大 出 口 與 海 外 專 案 進 入 量 產 ， 2026 年 成 長 明
顯 ， 毛 利 率 優 於 平 均 ， 2027 年 預 估 成 長 10 – 15% ， 提 供 穩 定 貢 獻 與 產
品 組 合 優 化 效 果 。  
d. 客戶 : 包括 GE Health car e 、Ph ilips 及 S iemens Healthin eers ， 產 品 滲 透
率 持 續 提 升 。

及 一 般 汽 車 電 子 訊 號 線（ 胎 壓 偵 測 、 360 度 環 景 、 倒 車 雷 達 等 ） 表 現 持 穩 ，
主 要 成 長 動能 來 自 控 制 器 ， 整 合 5 個 E CU 功 能 （ 含 引 擎 控 制 、 熱 管 理 、 電

YOY+10~15% 。

理 、 訊 號 控 制 等 ） 整 合 成 一 顆 「 大 控 制 器 」 ， 為 26~27 年 主 要 成 長 來 源 。  
b. 主要出貨給中國油電混合車客戶（以上海汽車體系為主，部分品牌有出
口 ） ， 並 擴 展 至 日 韓 等 市 場 。  
c. 展望 : 純 電 動 車 充 電 相 關 今 年 大 致 持 平，成 長 主 要 來 自 PD CU 控 制 器。2027
年 預 估 成 長 10 – 15% 。 長 期 受 惠 電 動 化 與 高 電 壓 ／ 大 電 流 解 決 方 案 需 求 。

年 增 約 28% ； 汽 車 年 增 約 27% 。 毛 利 率  24.5% （ 1Q26 為 24 .5% ） ， 產 品
組 合 優 化 部 分 抵 銷 機 櫃 組 裝 與 低 毛 利 汽 車 產 品 影 響 ， 本 期 淨 利 9.03 億元，
YOY+19% ，稅後 EPS  為 3.76 元。  
產 能 布 局 ：公 司 已 接 手 億 光 中 興 廠 區（ 預 計 2027 年 Q1 末 – Q2 初 投 產 ）、
苗 栗 新 廠（ 約 5,000 坪 無 塵 室， 2027 年 Q2 投 產 ） 、台 中 梧 棲 新 產 能（2027
年 Q3 ） 、 銅 鑼 科 學 園 區 新 廠 （ 2028 年 Q4 完工、 2029 年 出 貨 ， 專 攻 半 導
體 機 櫃 與 線 束 ） 。

評等說明

2)  買進 / Bu y： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於 25%  
3)  逢低買進 /Tr a din g Bu y ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 小 於 25%  
4)  未評等 / N ot Ra te d ： 至 目 前 我 們 沒 有 足 夠 基 本 資 料 判 斷 該 公 司 評 等 ； 或 此 期 間 康 和 與 該 公 司 有 特 定 交 易 ， 基 於 規 範 或 康
和 政 策 ， 限 制 予 該 公 司 評 等

免責宣言 :  
本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 康 和 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 ， 禁 止 任 何 形 式 之 抄 襲 、 引 用 或 轉 載 。 本 研 究 報 告 所 載 內 容 ， 純 屬
研 究 性 質 ， 所 載 述 意 見 可 隨 時 予 以 更 改 或 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 ， 僅 提 供 康 和 集 團 內 部 同 仁 及 客 戶 參 考 ， 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何
意 見 ， 不 構 成 ( 且 不 應 解 釋 為 ) 在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 劵 或 其 他 投 資 標 的 之 要 約 、 招 攬 、 邀 請 、 宣 傳 、 誘 使 ，
或 任 何 不 論 種 類 或 形 式 之 表 示 、 建 議 或 推 薦 買 賣 本 研 究 報 告 所 述 的 任 何 證 劵 。 報 告 內 容 所 提 及 之 各 項 業 務 、 財 務 等 相 關 資
訊 、 意 見 及 預 估 ， 係 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資 料 來 源 ， 既 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 做 出 任

報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 劵 、 投 資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 於 決 策 時 應 審 慎 衡 量 本 身 風 險 ， 並 就

3Q26 自結稅前淨利年增 27%，優於預期 7%  
鈊象 3Q26 營收 67.5 億元(季增 5.2%/年增 25.3%)，自結營業利益率季增 1.9
個百分點，反映高毛利授權收入佔比提升及營收規模擴大，帶動稅前淨利
42.9 億元(季增 8.6%/年增 27.4%)，優於我們/市場預期 7%/5%，推估 EPS 
12.55 元。展望 4Q26，9 月單月授權收入提升至 17.5 億元(vs 2025 年底 14
億元)，隨各國業務推進，估遊戲授權營收年增 33%，帶動整體營收年增
19.9%至 68.3 億元，預估 EPS 12.56 元(季持平/年增 18%)，上修 4%。 
美國跨州加速推進，估 2026/27 年遊戲授權年增 32%/15% 
網路遊戲授權為鈊象主要獲利成長引擎，預期 2026/27 年授權收入年增
32%/15%。以 1-8M26 區域別而言，1) 美國營收年增 65%、占比 14%，目
前全美共 35 個州可合法經營 Sweepstakes 相關業務，鈊象於深耕之三大州
市占率約 60%，過去三年營收表現平穩，近期透過新型 Social SS 拓展至其
他 32 州，將為未來數年最主要成長動能； 2) 東南亞營收年增 18%、占比
54%，雖基期已高，但考量市佔率低且部分地區仍有新增合作機會，預期維
持穩健成長；3) 其他地區營收年增 86%、占比 11%，南美洲近一年表現亮
眼、非洲自去年底進入市場後亦逐步取得進展、歐洲雖已取得多國執照且營
收持續成長，惟受國際大廠競爭、品牌知名度影響拓展速度相對較慢。 
海外授權多點開花，維持買進評等 
看好隨美國業務跨州加速推進、東南亞市場穩健成長，及 TaDa Gaming 品
牌在歐洲及其他新興市場持續擴張，帶動鈊象 2026/27 年營收年增 19%/10%；
隨高毛利遊戲授權業務佔比提升至 76%/79%、營運槓桿發酵，估營業利益
率年增 1.1 個/1.5 個百分點，EPS 年增 24.3%/11.5%至 47.88 元/53.38 元，
上修 3%/1%，評價維持歷史區間中值 20 倍本益比，以 2H26-1H27 EPS 
50.85 元(前次為 2026 年)，推得目標價 1,000 元，給予買進。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 遊戲授權營收年增 33%，佔比提升至 77%，使自結稅前淨利年增
27%，優於預期 7%；4Q26 授權收入持續疊加，估營收年增 20%。 
⧫  
美國跨州快速推進成為主要動能、東南亞穩健成長、TaDa Gaming 在
歐洲及新興市場持續擴張，估 2026/27 年授權收入年增 32%/15%。 
⧫  
看好鈊象高毛利授權占比提升、營運槓桿發酵，以 20 倍本益比及
2H26-1H27 EPS 50.85 元，推得目標價 1,000 元，維持買進評等。

### Page 2

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：軟體 2026 年 10 月 22 日 IGS 第2 頁，共 15頁  
營運分析  
3Q26 網路遊戲授權營收年增 43%，自結稅前淨利年增 27%，優於預期 7% 
鈊象 3Q26 營收 67.5 億元(季增 5.2%/年增 25.3%)，達成我們/市場預期 103%/101%，略優於預期，
營收佔比為網路遊戲授權 77%、網路遊戲 APP 21% 、商用遊戲機 2%，暑假遊戲旺季，且世界盃
投注熱潮外溢至其他線上博弈項目，帶動網路遊戲授權營收季增 8%/年增 43%；自結營業利益 41.6
億元(季增 8.5%/年增 34.3%)，營業利益率季增 1.9 個百分點至 61.7%，優於我們預期 1.8 個百分
點，反映高毛利授權收入佔比提升及營收規模擴大，自結稅前淨利 42.9 億元(季增 8.6%/年增
27.4%)，優於我們/市場預期 7%/5%，推估 EPS 12.55 元。 
三大業務 1-9M26 狀況如下： 
➢ 網路遊戲授權(營收佔比 77%)：累計營收年增 32%，美國及其他地區成長快速、東南亞穩健
擴張，單月遊戲授權收入提升至 17-18 億元，相對 2025 年底約 14 億元成長。 
➢ 網路遊戲 APP (21%) ：累計營收年減 6%，台灣市場已接近飽和及競爭激烈，且觀察 APP 玩
家付費率(ARPU)近年來維持在 1.5%至 2%之間，顯示休閒玩家的付費意願已達極限。 
➢ 商用遊戲機(2%)：累計營收年減 26%，營收佔比下滑，中國內需疲弱、競爭者新品推出快速，
且部分遊樂場停止經營使中古機台流入市場，壓抑新機需求。

授權收入持續疊加，估 4Q26 營收年增 20% 
9 月底鈊象單月遊戲授權收入提升至逾 17.5 億元(vs 2025 年底 14 億元)，隨公司於各國業務持續推
進，授權收入有望進一步擴張，估 4Q26 網路遊戲授權營收年增 33%至 53 億元，帶動整體營收年
增 19.9%至 68.3 億元，上修 2%，預估營業利益 42.1 億元(季增 1.1%/年增 24.5%)、稅後淨利 35.4
億元(季持平/年增 18%)，估 EPS 12.56 元，上修 4%。

海外版圖持續擴張，估 2026/27 年網路遊戲授權營收年增 32%/15% 
網路遊戲授權為鈊象主要獲利成長引擎，1-9M26 網路遊戲授權營收 145 億元，年增 32%，營收占
比自 2025 年之 68%提升至 75%，於遊戲授權業務毛利率達 100%，進一步推動公司毛利率提升。 
以 1-8M26 區域別而言， 1) 美國營收年增 65%、占比 14%，目前全美共  35 個州可合法經營
Sweepstakes 相關業務，鈊象於深耕之三大州市占率約 60%，過去三年營收表現平穩，近期透過新
型 Social SS 拓展至其他 32 州，將為未來數年最主要成長動能，同時我們認為於美國更長遠成長
引擎，將來自跨足市場規模數倍於 SS 領域的 online casino 市場；2) 東南亞營收年增 18%、占比
維持54%，雖基期已高，但考量市佔率低且部分地區仍有新增合作機會，預期維持穩健成長； 3) 其
他地區(排除台灣、中國、美國、東南亞)營收年增 86%、占比 11%，以 TaDa Gaming 品牌業務為
主，其中南美洲近一年表現亮眼、非洲自去年底進入市場後亦逐步取得進展、歐洲為全球最大線上
博弈市場，雖已取得多國執照且營收持續成長，惟受國際大廠競爭、品牌知名度影響拓展速度相對
較慢，公司正積極擴大代理商合作以加速產品上架排程。 
整體而言，隨美國業務跨州加速推進、東南亞市場穩健貢獻，及 TaDa Gaming 品牌在歐洲及其他
新興市場持續擴張，預期鈊象 2026/27 年網路授權遊戲收入 198 億/229 億元，年增 32%/15%。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：軟體 2026 年 10 月 22 日 IGS 第5 頁，共 15頁  
產業概況  
全球線上博弈市場規模將以 CAGR 10.5%成長至 2034 年 2,864 億美元 
根據 Facts and Factors 2026/2月報告， 2025 年全球線上博弈市場規模約 1,055 億美元，預計將以
CAGR 10.5% 擴張至 2034 年達 2,864 億美元，動能包括多國政府積極推動線上博弈合法化，以爭
取稅收增長、行動裝置及 5G 網路普及、數位支付機制成熟，以及 AI 技術在玩家行為分析與精準行
銷上的廣泛應用。按照遊戲類型劃分，體育博弈佔比最高達 48%、其次為線上賭場；按照區域劃分，
歐洲佔 41%為最大的線上博弈市場，歸因於多國具備極為完善且透明的監管體系、北美多州逐步開
放線上博弈，帶動市場成長、拉丁美洲與雅泰地區因基期低及滲透率提升，預期將快速成長。  
圖 7：2021-2034 年全球線上博弈市場規模預測

資料來源：Global market insight、元大投顧 
社交博弈不涉及現金提現，純娛樂性質降低法規監管門檻 
社交博弈不同於傳統線上博弈，其核心在於不涉及現金提現的純娛樂性質，主要受網路遊戲法規監
管，根據 The Business Research Company《2026 年社交博弈全球市場報告》，2025 年全球社交
博弈市場規模為 92.4 億美元，預計將以 CAGR 9.1% 持續成長，至 2030 年達 144 億美元，動能來
自全球網路普及率提升進而擴大博弈遊戲的受眾，此外業者積極開發創新的技術平台與互動機制，
有效提升社交博弈市場的獲利。在區域表現方面，北美為全球最大社交博弈市場，亞太地區受惠於
人口紅利與網路設備普及，預期將成為成長最快速的區域。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：軟體 2026 年 10 月 22 日 IGS 第6 頁，共 15頁  
全球遊戲市場 2025-2028 年 CAGR 達 4.9%，PC 遊戲成長最強勁 
根據 Newzoo 2025/12 遊戲產業市場報告，2025 年全球遊戲市場總收入預計達 1,967 億美元，年增 
7.5%，成長動能來自既有玩家更強的付費深度與更長的時間投入。細分平台市場，手機遊戲 (佔比
55%)年增 7.7%，受益於成熟的線上服務與廣大的全球覆蓋率、PC 遊戲(22%)年增 10.4%，受惠於
高品質遊戲的密集發行，以及 Steam 平台在亞洲市場滲透率提升、主機遊戲(23%)年增 4.2%，受
惠硬體設備更替及指標性作品推出。 
展望未來，預估全球遊戲產業 2025-2028 年將以 4.9%的 CAGR 成長至 2,272 億美元，並以 PC 遊
戲的 6.6%的 CAGR 表現最為強勁，其市場規模有望於 2028 年超越主機遊戲，主要受惠 Steam 降
低遊戲開發及供給門檻、東亞與新興市場玩家數量增加，以及於數位原生代 (Z 世代和 Alpha 世代)
成為消費主力。 
圖 9：2022-2028 年全球遊戲市場規模預測

資料來源：Newzoo、元大投顧 
台灣遊戲市場維持成長趨勢，但付費意願下滑 
根據資誠《2023-2027 全球娛樂暨媒體業展望》報告指出， 2024 年台灣電玩遊戲與電競產業產值
約達 39 億美元，2022 年至 2027 年 CAGR 達 4.8%，至 2027 年產值達 44.6 億美元，主要動能來
自 1) 智慧型手機滲透率高；2) 高速數據傳輸方案價格親民；3) 5G 覆蓋率持續提升等。在付費狀況
方面，台灣在全球行動遊戲營收中，每使用者平均收入 (ARPU)長期位居全球前十名，根據資策會
統計，台灣網友遊玩數位遊戲的滲透率已高達 81.4%，但受疫後消費回歸線下、通膨及影音社群等
多元娛樂排擠資源影響，2025 年整體玩家具付費習慣的比例下降至 35.5%，較前一年下滑約一成，
反應消費者對免費內容的依賴度提升，在付費決策上更趨於精打細算，未來市場的獲利動能在於透
過精準的數據分析與高黏著度的內容經營，深挖核心付費族群的長期價值。

速推進、東南亞市場穩健貢獻，及 TaDa Gaming 品牌在歐洲及其他新興市場持續擴張，估帶動網

鈊象歷史三年交易於 10-30 倍本益比，看好公司海外遊戲授權業務保持擴張態勢，全球線上博弈市
場規模 2025 至 2034 年將以 CAGR 10.5% 成長，鈊象於既有市場擴大市佔，並持續開拓新市場，
帶動獲利逐年走升，此次評價維持歷史區間中值 20 倍本益比，以 2H26-1H27 EPS 50.85 元(前次
為 2026 年)，推得目標價 1,000 元，給予買進評等。

公司簡介  
鈊象成立於 1989 年，為台灣商用遊戲機與網路遊戲大廠，專注於遊戲軟硬體之設計、研發、製造與行銷，初期以發展商用
遊戲機為主，擁有出色的 IP 如《三國戰記》和《西遊》等。然而為分散中國街機市場競爭和高收入集中風險，鈊象 2000 年
跨入 PC 與線上遊戲產業，推出經典麻將線上遊戲「明星三缺一」、「唯舞獨尊」及「封神 Online」等，為公司的社交娛樂
場產品組合奠定堅實的基礎。2010 年起在智慧手機崛下時空背景下，鈊象 2015 年線上遊戲業務比重正式超越商用遊戲機，
而近五年隨自營 APP 產品玩家人數逐漸飽和，鈊象發展重心轉往海外授權，與當地代理商合作收取授權金以及服務費。
2025 年營收比重為：網路授權遊戲 68%、網路遊戲 APP 29%、商用遊戲機 2%。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：軟體 2026 年 10 月 22 日 IGS 第12 頁，共 15頁

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分：鈊象整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排行在略微領先的位置，於
娛樂軟體業的公司中排名落後同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險：鈊象的整體曝險較低，略低於行業平均。人力資本、資料隱私與網路安全以及商業道德是值得關
注的重要 ESG 議題。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：鈊象在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬平均水準。該公司展現出非常強而
有力的環境政策和嚴格的 ESG 報告標準。其舉報人保護機制尚可完善，但仍有提升空間與保護機制的必要性。 ESG 治
理水準適中，設有 ESG 相關委員會，但其權限低於董事會。

分析師聲明 
主要負責撰寫本研究報告全文或部分內容之分析師，茲針對本報告所載證券或證券發行機構，於此聲明： (1) 文中所述觀點皆準確反映其個人對各證券或
證券發行機構之看法；(2) 研究部分析師於本研究報告中所提出之特定投資建議或觀點，與其過去、現在、未來薪酬的任何部份皆無直接或間接關聯。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

本報告僅提供一般資訊，文中所載資訊或任何意見，並不構成任何買賣證券或其他投資標的之要約或要約之引誘。報告資料之刊發僅供客戶一般傳閱用途，
並非意欲提供專屬之投資建議，亦無考慮任何可能收取本報告之人士的個別財務狀況與目標。對於投資本報告所討論或建議之任何證券、投資標的，或文
中所討論或建議之投資策略，投資人應就其是否適合本身而諮詢財務顧問的意見。本報告之內容取材自據信為可靠之資料來源，但概不以明示或默示的方

1 
6770 力積電(Powerchip)

報告日期： 2026/10/22 研 究 員：羅  金 鳳 (Sunny Lo)  
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 3Q26 在記憶體及邏輯產品價格季增 20% 的帶動下，使整體毛
利率跳升 19 個百分點 ， 來到47.69% ，單 季 稅 後EPS 1.38 元，
優於預期。  
 4Q26 將反映晶圓投片及產出的時間差，使出貨量及 ASP 將再
增加，帶動營收、毛利率雙位升。  
 AI 帶動矽電容需求大爆發，目前力積電產能僅 1 萬片 /月，但
2027 年需求將達 3～5 萬片 / 月。

一. 反映投片與產出落差， 4Q26 營收、毛利率雙升  
力積電 3Q26 晶圓出貨量 41.6 萬片， QoQ-1.44% ，主因為銅鑼廠
機器搬遷所致，且 3D AI Foundry 營收占比也因遷廠而由 2Q26
的 5.4% 下滑到 1.4%， 但因記憶體及邏輯產品價格持續上漲 ，ASP
季增 20% ，使力積電 3Q26 整體營收達 212.78 億元，
QoQ+23.07% ，YoY+79.71% ，在價格上漲及產能利用率提升的帶
動下，整體毛利率由 2Q26 的 28.28% 大幅上升至 47.69% ，稅後
EPS 1.38 元，優於預期。

由於力積電投片與產出的落差 30%( 邏輯遞延 2～3 個月， DRAM
遞延 4 個月 )，因此， 4Q26 的晶圓出貨及 ASP 將比 3Q26 更高，
加上 ， 銅鑼廠的機器設備已完成搬遷到新竹廠 ，Interposer 及 WoW
的產能將在 11～12 月逐步恢復生產，其中 Interposer 將於 2027
年達到滿載。綜上，我們預估力積電 4Q26 營收 234.3 億元，
QoQ+10.11% ，YoY+87.51% ，毛利率進一步提升至 51.81% ，單
季稅後 EPS 1.64 元，合計 2026 年營收 755.71 億元，
YoY+61.72% ，在記憶體價格大漲的帶動下，毛利率由 2025 年的
-3.29% 跳升至 37.8% ，稅後 EPS 6.58 元(含售廠給美光 )。

二. 2027 年矽電容需求大爆發  
力積電營運轉型將陸續展現成果，美光授權生產的 DRAM 的 1P
製程，將於 2026 完成 PDK ，2Q27 試產， 2028 年有機會貢獻全
年營收； HBM 的後段製程代工 PWF ，由美光提供設備， 2026 年
試產， 4Q27 量產， 2028 年貢獻全年營收； 3D AI Foundry 方面，
堆疊技術將由 Micro bump 進入到 Hybrid bonding ，以及
Interposer 的放量生產 ， 在外溢效果持續下 ，2027 年產能將滿載；
Wafer on Wafer 方面，在銅鑼廠設千完全搬遷至新竹廠後，目前
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：95 元 
     11 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：66.2 元 
     7.67 X 2027 EPS 
     2026/10/21 收盤

公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

2 
已小量試產， 1H27 將正式量產。由於建廠及買購設備需大額資本
支出，風險相對較高，因此， PWF 由美光提供設備，未來的 WoW
也將會採取輕資產的模式，降低營運風險，不過，因大廠均將產
能移去生產高階 DRAM ，38nm 、25nm 、20nm 這些傳統 DRAM
製程未來全球將只剩力積電生產，預估此一需求將長達 10～15
年。

此外 ， 因AI 晶片的高功耗及高速傳輸需求 ， 為提升電源傳輸效率 、
降低電阻、去雜訊等問題，將帶動 IPD 需求大爆發，力積電目前
以現有 DRAM 的 38nm、25nm 製程做生產 IPD，恰好目前 DRAM
產能供不應求的情況下 ，IPD 的產能只能配置約 1 萬片 /月，而2027
年全球 IPD 的需求將達 3～5 萬片 /月，且能見度已延伸至 2028
年，顯見需求遠超過力積電所能提供，因此，力積電已在尋找建
廠用地，且將聯合策略客戶共同投資新產能。

三. 預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 8.63 元 
AI 進入到推理後對記憶體已需求量更甚以往，但因全球記憶體產
能嚴重供不應求的情況下， GUP 大廠將 12 層 HBM 降低到 8 層，
顯見記憶體緊俏情況嚴峻，也使記憶體價格易漲難跌，預估 2027
年記憶體價格仍至少維持在高點，加上成熟製程處在有利的價格
環境， 2026 年漲價雙位數後， 2027 年將再漲 10～20% ，因此，
我們預估力積電 2027 年營收 1223.38 億元， YoY+61.89% ，毛利
率 49.89% ，稅後 EPS 8.63 元。

四. 投資建議為買進，目標價 95 元 
受惠 AI 帶動的記憶體漲價潮持續， 2026 年力積電將呈現轉虧為
盈，且 2026 及 2027 年獲利都將大幅成長，加上銅鑼廠售予美光
且變成長期的代工合作夥伴關係，有利力積電長期營運穩定性，
也解決力積電資產負債表壓力，因此，投資建議為買進，目標價
95 元(11X2027 年稅後 EPS) 。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介  
力晶集團 05/2019 完成企業重組 ， 由旗下的力晶科技3 座 12 吋晶
圓廠及相關營業、資產，讓與力晶積成電子製造股份有限公司 (簡
稱“力積電” (6770)) 。力積電為聚焦專業晶圓代工、產業定位明
確，目前擁有 2 座 8 吋及 3 座 12 吋晶圓廠，將針對先進記憶體、
客製化邏輯積體電路與分離式元件的三大晶圓代工服務主軸，持
續 Open Foundry 營運模式，從晶片設計、製造服務，到設備、

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

3 
產能分享，根據不同客戶的屬性和需求，共同建立緊密、彈性的
合作機制，且著眼半導體 3D 堆疊產業大趨勢，力積電與國際知名
大客戶密切配合開發 3D 堆疊封裝，將成為公司長線另一成長動
能。

二.產業趨勢概況  
1、佈局 3D IC 為公司未來運重要利基 
力積電有部份產品屬於記憶體產品，營運雖然將隨著市場波動，
受產業景氣循環大，然力積電善用其技術優勢，將其記憶體產品
與國際知名大廠合作，延伸至 3D 堆疊封裝技術，僅需利用舊製程
即可將晶片效能數倍提升，其以 55nm 的邏輯 IC 堆疊 38nm 的
DRAM 製程，封裝後的晶片用於挖礦機，效能較現有的解決方案
大幅提升 9 倍。由 於 透 過3D 堆疊可使晶片設計效率提高， 提升晶
片整體效能，減少晶片尺寸，加快晶片內部元件溝通速度，為半
導體先進封裝未來重要發展趨勢，且因先進封裝技術成本不斷提
升，客戶為考量使用的成本效益，未來將有更多廠商使用 3D 堆疊
的解決方案取代先進製程技術 ， 使得3D 堆疊技術長線發展具極大
潛力，力積電長線著眼於此一產業大趨勢，將積極深化此一佈局，
為公司長線營運提供良好動能。

2、四大營運方向引領公司未來成長動能 
力積電在經過 2019 年 3 座 8 吋廠及 2 座 12 吋廠的整併，加上邏
輯技術逐漸步上軌道後，使其在 40nm ～0.11um 的技術於全球站
穩腳步，具有指標地位。公司除將以此為基礎拓展多元應用，在
新技術方面亦不斷投入研發，展望未來，擬定四大方向引領公司
成長動能： 1、發展更先進的技術 --- 第四代半導體氧化銦鎵鋅
(IGZO) ，因 IGZO 的材料特性，其超低功耗的性能，僅為目前耗
電的 1/20， 及顯示器解析度能超過5,000DDIP ， 有助應用於AR/VR
的裝置有較佳續航力及超高解析度，使用者將完全沈浸於元宇宙
世界。 2、3D-Interchip 堆疊技術：與晶圓代工大廠台積電合作，
將記憶體與邏輯 IC 進行 3D 堆疊 ， 使晶片具更小體積 、 更低功耗，
更低成本，及開發更有效率。 3D 堆疊的 Wafer on Wafer 目前以
供應挖礦機需求為主，未來將專注於 AI 龐大需求。 3、Interchip ：
將邏輯元件直接放入記憶體元件，以提高記憶體效能，適用於
IoT 、AI 及邊緣運算。 4、車用電子晶片：因應節能減碳的趨勢，
電動車 / 新能源車將快速發展，公司將致力推進第三代半導體
GaN、SiC 技術製程，應用於 PMIC 。由於 PMIC 為車汽應用的重
要零組件之一，且 PMIC 的製造非用最先進的半導體製程，力積
電目前的製程範圍恰好符合最佳生產製程的節點，而電動車的興
起將帶動 PMIC 的應用大幅成長，加上 AI 趨勢確立下，對 PMIC
有更高需求，為公司未來重要佈局方向及營運重要成長動能。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클SK하이닉스(000660) 높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리_0a1416f9.pdf

### Page 1

2026년 10월 29일 
기업분석

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(L TA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 L TA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 L TA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 L TA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 29 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성됐음을 확인합니다. 
본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 자료를 
기관투자자 또는 제3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (박준영, 김나우) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

주가 추이

3분기 영업 이 익 은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일 회성 비용 등의 영 향 으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복 과 중 장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸 류 에이 션 에 주목합 니 다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계 
금융투자상품의 비중 96.4% 3.6% 0.0% 100.0%

Document 248: Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Advanced economies _ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
History tends to repeat itself in advanced economies. Once again, growth ultimately fell short of expectations by only a small
margin in the first half of 2026, despite the conflict in Iran. As early as 2025, the impact of tariffs was less severe than feared.
This is a sign that structural factors (notably AI, defence and electrification) are underpinning growth in these countries

and are expected to continue to do so in 2027. However, there are significant risks to the economic outlook, primarily of a
geopolitical nature, with a significant upside risk to inflation and interest rates. The other instance of history repeating itself
concerns a period that those under 20 cannot have experienced: the period leading up to the 2008 crisis, echoes of which can
be seen in terms of growth, inflation, the direction of monetary policy, and long-term interest rates.

Advanced economies are experiencing trends similar to those they
faced nearly 20 years ago:
- Growth remains resilient in 2025-26 despite shocks, as it did in 2006-
07, in the major advanced economies.
- Inflation is above central banks’ targets: as in 2006-07, this calls for a
tightening of monetary policy.
- In Europe and the United States, long-term interest rates have re -
turned to their 2006-07 levels (1996 in Japan): a challenging environ-

ment for public finances in a context where public debt ratios are signi-
ficantly higher than those seen 20 years ago.
THE GLOBAL ECONOMY IN THE MIDST OF A GROWTH CYCLE
We are already well into 2026, and the picture so far is clear: glo -
bal growth is proving resilient. Following a US tariff shock last year,
growth is once again proving, for the time being, to be significantly less
affected by the conflict in Iran than had been anticipated when it first

broke out. Initially, however, there were fears that this new shock would
hit growth even harder. As with the tariff hike in 2025, the impact was
measurable, particularly in terms of consumer confidence. But in both
2025 and 2026, growth has also shown a degree of resilience.
The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise
in oil prices, the recessionary impact of which is not yet apparent.
According to our forecasts, growth was expected to reach or exceed 1%

in 2026 in the four major advanced economies (the Eurozone, Japan,
the United Kingdom and the United States, where it exceeds 2%) for
the second consecutive year. This had not happened (excluding the
post-COVID catch-up period) since 2006-07. Growth figures are more
moderate (particularly for demographic reasons), but the private sec -
tor is not (at present) showing the same excesses (at that time, the
credit boom had preceded the worst crisis since the 1930s); although

debt levels have recently risen in the US tech sector.
The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial
intelligence (AI). It is fuelling global trade and business investment
in most countries. This is clearly the case in the United States, where
business investment is growing strongly as investment plans in the
US tech sector are implemented.
However, the world’s major economies are currently engaged in a
broader push to expand their capital goods investment. Alongside the

ongoing development of services—particularly digital services—we
have seen a rebound in industrial production in most countries since
early 2025. The leading sectors are primarily technology equipment,
machinery, aeronautics, defence and their essential inputs (energy and
metals). This trend is evident everywhere, including in Europe, and
is becoming entrenched over the long term, with an improvement in
business climate indicators, particularly new export orders. Growth is

therefore expected to strengthen further in 2027, particularly in the
Eurozone, where German investment plans are set to gather momen -
tum and whose spillover effects will have a greater impact on the eco-
nomy.
However, not all sectors are experiencing the same momentum. This
is particularly true for the sectors that depend on household consump-
tion, which has shown mixed trends. Our observation that consumer
spending is more buoyant in the United States than in Europe was

confirmed in 2026, and the outlook does not point to a change in this
ranking.

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
While European and US households are experiencing more or less the
same energy price shock, inflation remains higher in the United States.
But this has not prevented consumer spending from keeping pace, pri-
marily because it is driven by the wealthiest households, who are less
sensitive to inflation than to the wealth effect. Furthermore, while the

savings rate remains high in the Eurozone, US households have been
drawing down their savings. This is also true of British households,
which explains why the UK has seen slightly stronger growth since
the beginning of the year. In Japan, government spending has partially
offset energy inflation (while other countries are doing so to a much
lesser extent than in 2022), which has supported consumption growth.
However, these buffers—reduced savings or fiscal support—have their

limits when shocks persist. The two major current conflicts (Iran and
Ukraine) are far from being resolved, and their impact is continuing.
This persistent geopolitical risk and its implications for inflation repre-
sent a significant headwind to the growth outlook for 2027.
HIGHER INFLATION CALLS FOR MONETARY TIGHTENING
Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks
examined in this publication, which is another similarity (albeit with

different underlying causes) with the 2006-07 period. Japan is the ex -
ception, as government support measures for households are tempo -
rarily holding back inflation.
Apart from the Bank of Japan, which is engaged in a long-term norma-
lisation process, no central bank was expected to raise its key rates
in 2026. But they are now being prompted to do so for reasons that
vary from one region to another. The first is the conflict in Iran and
the resulting rise in oil prices, which triggered a rebound in energy

inflation from the second quarter onwards. However, since the conflict
was not resolved by the memorandum of understanding signed in June,
and given that the subsequent drop in oil prices proved short-lived,
inflation is expected to rise again. The rise in gas prices is expected to
exacerbate the impact, particularly in Europe, where inventories are
being replenished ahead of winter. Electricity prices are expected to
follow suit in most countries, but at a more moderate pace than in

2022. Indeed, gas is now used less to generate electricity than it was
at that time (thanks to the expansion of renewable energy), and to an
even lesser extent in France.
There is a significant risk that energy inflation will spread further to
other components of inflation in the future, and inflation expectations
could eventually reflect this. This is why the European Central Bank
has already raised its key interest rate twice (in June and September)

and, according to our scenario, will likely do so again in December. For
the same reason, we expect the Bank of England to raise its policy rate
in November. Although the cap on energy prices was maintained for
a long time, it has now been raised. As a result, inflation is expected
to rise between now and the end of the year. The aim will then be to
prevent second-round effects.
Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength. In the

United States, it has exceeded the 2% target since 2021. The main
argument in favour of a new cycle of rate hikes (following the one in
2022-2023) lies in the current resilience of growth and the improving
labour market. The Federal Reserve (Fed) must balance two equally
important objectives: maximum employment and price stability. Howe-
ver, unlike in the 4 th quarter of 2025, when the decline in job crea -
tion justified monetary easing (by 75 basis points), the current sharp

rebound in job creation completely reverses that analysis. According
to our forecasts, the unemployment rate is expected to decrease in
the coming months, which pushes back the prospect of a decline in
core inflation. This situation persists due to both supply constraints
(over which a central bank has no control) and excess demand (which
the Fed can, however, mitigate). Consequently, the Fed raised its key
rate in September and is expected to implement two further rate hikes

between now and January 2027.
In Japan, finally, while headline inflation has been brought down by
government support measures, core inflation remains significant.
Wage pressures are mounting against a backdrop of labour shortages.
The Bank of Japan is therefore being forced to accelerate the pace of its
monetary normalisation.
In all these regions, inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026
or Q1 2027. It is against this backdrop of temporary inflation that rate

hikes could also come to an end by that time (excluding Japan). Howe-
ver, this assumes that once the energy shock—the course of which
remains uncertain—subsides, inflation will fall. The effects of El Niño,
which are still difficult to gauge at present, could take over. Inflation
would then remain above the central banks’ target for a longer period.
Under this scenario, rate hikes would go further than is currently en -
visaged.
THE RISE IN LONG- TERM INTEREST RATES: A FACTOR THAT WILL WEIGH

ON PUBLIC FINANCES
Monetary policies have also to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal
policy, particularly in the United States (where the budget deficit is
high and shows no signs of shrinking) and in Japan (where the new
government is pursuing pro-growth policies). In Europe, fiscal policy
is playing a much smaller role than it did in 2022. While the stimulus
measures implemented at that time did help reduce the inflation rate
in the short term (by nearly 2.5 pp in France, for example), by keeping

demand at a high level, they delayed disinflation and increased the
scale of the monetary tightening required.
The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: it triggered a
rise in long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the
start of the year) to levels close to those of 2006-07. This rise is wides-
pread, but its causes vary. Inflation expectations appear to play a more
significant role in the Eurozone, whereas the rise in real interest rates

seems to be the main factor in the United States and Japan.

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
Higher inflation and more resilient real growth than feared point in
the same direction: stronger than expected nominal growth that is li -
kely to be sustained. However, in the long term, high nominal growth
tends to drive up nominal interest rates (as it is a positive sign of more
profitable investment projects), in contrast to the problem of secular

stagnation raised in the 2010s (which combined low nominal growth
with low nominal interest rates).
The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period
of growth), with various situations. In several countries, the public
debt-to-GDP ratio continues to rise. In the United States, deficit reduc-
tion does not appear to be a priority, to the extent that it is expected
to remain above 6% of GDP at the very least until the end of Donald

Trump’s term of office (with little hope that the mid-term elections will
significantly alter this trend). In the United Kingdom and France, fiscal
consolidation has begun, but it is being hampered by the simultaneous
increase in certain areas of expenditure (debt servicing, defence and
healthcare, due in particular to an ageing population), which is slowing
the pace of consolidation. In Germany, the implementation of invest -
ment plans is leading to a widening of the deficit. While this does not

pose a sustainability problem, it does result in an increase in annual
debt issuance. In Japan, the budget deficit is kept in check by interest
payments that remain low, as the rise in interest rates is recent and
the average maturity of the debt is high (10 years). However, expansio-
nary fiscal policy could pose further problems in terms of debt dyna -
mics if it were to continue through the end of the decade. In Italy and
Spain, fiscal consolidation has been sufficient to stabilise the public

debt-to-GDP ratio (and even to reduce it in Spain), but the high level of
Italy’s debt service it vulnerable to a rise in interest rates.
In all these countries, the fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it
will be in 2030. Indeed, in most cases, only half of the debt was is -
sued after 2022 (that is, after the end of the zero-interest-rate period).
The debt service has so far only partially risen, except in the United
States, where most of the increase has already occurred due to a shor-

ter average debt maturity (6 years). This means that, for all countries
combined, the rate of GDP growth remains higher than the average
interest rate paid on public debt (or the effective interest rate).
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with
GDP growth. As a result, in most cases, the debt service will have re -
turned to the level as a share of GDP seen in the early 2000s. In order to
stabilise public debt-to-GDP ratios, primary balances will need to have

improved. This will even require the generation of primary surpluses,
particularly in Italy (which is already achieving this), France and the
United Kingdom. This is a crucial shift that governments will need to
introduce as early as the 2027 budget; otherwise, they risk postponing
the stabilisation of their public debt-to-GDP ratio once again.
Article completed on 17 September 2026
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

ADVANCED ECONOMIES
In brief. The week was dominated by a continued global selloff in tech
stocks, leading to safe haven flows into US Treasuries, Japanese Go -
vernment bonds and German bunds. As a result, their yields fell des -
pite energy prices surging to or past their Spring highs. Economic data
generally showed continued resilience. French bonds were under rising

pressure, but Moody's unexpected decision to maintain its France ra -
ting unchanged could, at the margin, help stabilise the spread. The Fed,
ECB and BOJ should be on hold next week while signaling further rate
action is likely.
EUROPEAN UNION
A win for securitization. The Council and the European Parliament have
reached a provisional political agreement on measures to revive and
simplify the EU’s securitization market. The updated framework will

reduce banks’ capital requirements for the safest and most well-struc-
tured transactions, notably for a newly introduced “resilient” category
of securitization. As a key component of the “One Europe, One Market”
initiative and the EU’s Savings and Investment Union agenda, this aims
to channel increased lending to households and businesses. The rele -
vant legal texts must now be finalised.
EUROZONE
Expansion continues. The flash PMIs weakened in October, mainly on

the back of a decline in France. The EZ composite index dipped to 51.9
(-1.2 m/m), dragged down by services (51.7, -1.3 m/m), although ma -
nufacturing also dipped (52.4, -0.5 m/m). Both remain in expansion
territory, and manufacturing new export orders rose to their highest
level since early 2022. Price signals were also rather reassuring, with
the services output price index easing to 54.9 (-1.7 m/m). Households
are more cautious: consumer confidence fell for a second consecutive

month to a five-month low (-17.9). Households’ inflation expectations
edged up at short horizons (+0.1 pp to 3.6% at one year and 3.1% at
three years), but long-term expectations remained anchored, with the
five-year median stable at 2.5%.
France
Rising market stress despite decent economic news; a reprieve from
Moody’s. The spread on French 10-year bond relative to Germany’s
peaked to 174 bps on Friday, the highest since 2011 and only 16 bps
short of its all-time high, as markets price in anticipation of worst-case

scenarios regarding the country’s ability to repair its public finances.
The 10Y yield briefly exceeded 5.1% before falling back to 5% (above
the US equivalent). Nonetheless, services output was stable in August,
following a 0.6% m/m increase in July, a much higher pace than in Q2.
The rebound is driven by the catering sector, the digital sector, business
services, and real estate. The INSEE business climate improved from
96 to 97 (long-term average = 100) in October, but the survey was

conducted before the riots. The main improvement was in manufactu-
ring, which rose from 101 to 105, driven by increased new orders in the
aeronautics sector, and to a lesser extent by the services sector (from
98 to 99). The PMI survey (conducted partially after the riots) was
less positive: the composite index fell to 48.4 (-2.7 m/m), with a sharp
decline in services (48.4; -2.8 m/m) and a slightly smaller decline in
manufacturing (49.3; -1.3 m/m). Moody's unexpectedly announced af -

ter the market close on Friday that it was maintaining France’s ra -
ting at Aa3 on account of France’s diversified economic structure and
sound public institutions, but maintained its negative outlook. This is
on par with the UK and one notch above S&P and Fitch. Moody’s made
clear keeping this rating is conditional on a budget being adopted that
reduces the fiscal deficit, along with an easing of social and market
stress going forward. Saturday’s demonstrations across the country

saw strong attendance but only limited violent incidents.
Germany
Prices continue to rise, but confidence surveys remain on an upward
trend. Producer prices rose 6.7% y/y in September (+2.1pp m/m; the
highest level in 3.5 years) due to higher prices for energy and inter -
mediate goods, primarily metals. The ZEW Economic Sentiment Index
improved beyond consensus forecasts in October and returned to its
highest level since May 2023 (-35.9; +11.2 m/m), albeit the Economic

Expectations Index fell to its lowest level since July (28.1; -6.6 m/m).
The recovery in economic activity continues, although a slowdown is
evident: the flash Composite PMI slowed (52.8; -1 m/m; the lowest in
2 months) due to weaker growth in the services sector (51.7; -1.1 m/m;
the lowest in 2 months) and in manufacturing output (55.1; -0.9 m/m;
the lowest in 3 months). Nevertheless, in both sectors, hiring is rising
slightly, supported by growth in export sales and the spillover effects of

investment plans. Despite persistently high input prices, selling prices
are slowing (reaching their lowest level since February in the manu -
facturing sector).
UNITED KINGDOM
Inflation, wage growth and activity accelerate. CPI inflation overshot
consensus on energy prices, printing at 0.4% m/m and 3.5% y/y in Sep-
tember (consensus: 3.4%, prior: 3.1%), its highest in 11 months. Core
CPI came in at 2.8% y/y (up 0.2 pp), in line with consensus. Pressures

remained persistent according to the PPI at 8.3% y/y (prior: 6.7%). La -
bour market and activity data also surprised positively. Payroll growth
reached 102k in August (3m/3m), bringing the unemployment rate to
4.7% (down 0.2 pp), its lowest since July 2025. October PMI surveys
beat expectations in both manufacturing (52.8, consensus: 51.8, prior:
51.9) and services (53.1, consensus: 51.8, prior: 52.1). The only negative
news came from headline retail sales, falling 0.4% m/m in September,

with the core gauge (excluding petrol) deteriorating by -0.7% m/m.
UNITED STATES
Construction struggling, GENIUS Act implemented. Housing starts
dropped -5.2% m/m in September (-7.4% y/y). This third consecutive
monthly decline dragged the Q3 average to its lowest since Q2 2020.
Building permits also came in negative, at -0.2% m/m (-3.0% y/y), while
the NAHB homebuilder sentiment index plunged to its weakest since
2012. The average 30-year fixed mortgage rate eased to 7.32% (still

134bp above pre-war level), as the ongoing AI selloff pushed down
US treasury yields. The GENIUS Act final implementation rule from the
Office of the Comptroller of the Currency’s (OCC) leaves the door open
for stablecoin customers to receive yield or rewards. Issuers remain
prohibited from paying holders any form of interest or yield solely
in connection with the holding, use, or retention of payment stable -
coins. This represents a shift from the OCC’s February proposal and

has renewed concerns within the banking industry about deposit flight.
The rule will take effect on January 18, 2027.
JAPAN
Activity and inflation keep rising. Composite PMI was unchanged in
September at 52.3, solidly in expansion territory, as an improvement
in services activity offset slower output growth in manufacturing. The
Manufacturing PMI disappointed at 53.3 (-0.8 m/m; an 8-month low).
Underlying price pressures, meanwhile, extended. Headline CPI was

flat on a monthly basis, bringing the annual rate up to 2.1% (up 0.4 pp).
Core CPI (excl. fresh food) matched it at 2.1% y/y (up 0.4 pp), in line
with consensus. More importantly, the New Core index, which strips
out fresh food and energy, rose sharply to 2.4% y/y (up 0.5 pp), its
fastest pace since January.
EMERGING ECONOMIES

ASIA
China
New monetary policy tool and export controls. The central bank’s Go-
vernor Pan Gongsheng unveiled a plan to switch (“when appropriate”)
its key policy rate from the 7-day reverse repo rate (at 1.4% since May
2025) to the overnight reverse repo rate, in order to reflect develop -
ments in the interbank market. New export controls: The government
plans to add 40 key technologies to the list of technologies prohibited
or restricted for export, including in the AI (for ex. LLMs, computing

clusters), biotechnology and photovoltaic sectors.
Indonesia
BI kept policy rate unchanged at 5.75%, raised its deposit facility rate
by 25bp to 5% and announced a phased cut in reserve requirements
from 9% to 7% by December. This combination of limited FX support
and measures to support credit growth sends mixed signals amidst
pressures on the rupiah following MSCI’s downgrade warning and un-
dermines the central bank’s credibility. The rupiah has lost 1% against

the USD since mid-October, with little contagion effect on other Asian
currencies.
South Korea
The Bank of Korea (BoK) kept its policy rate unchanged at 3.0%, but
the tightening cycle may not be over. CPI inflation was 2.9% y/y in Sep-
tember, above the central bank’s target of 2%, while high oil prices,
rapid growth in Seoul housing prices and wage pressures (though
concentrated in the electronics sector) raise the risk of lasting infla -
tion pressure. As economic activity remains robust, supported by strong

exports and recovering investment, the BoK has scope to raise rates
further.
EMEA
Hungary
Monetary status quo and 2027 budget in consolidation mode. The
Central Bank kept its policy rate unchanged at 5.5%, as expected. A cau-
tious stance will likely prevail in the short term. Inflation has been
picking up in recent months, albeit mildly, and will continue to move
upward as the government recently removed the margin cap on fuel.
Separately, the Budget for 2027 and the medium-term fiscal plan show

Hungary’s goal to join the Eurozone; the government expects to reduce
the fiscal deficit to 4.9% of GDP in 2027 (from 7% expected in 2026) and
towards 2.8% of GDP by 2030.
Türkiye
Cautious monetary loosening. The Central Bank lowered its policy rate
by 100 bp to 36% at its October Monetary Policy Committee meeting,
in line with market expectations. The overnight lending and borrowing
rates were also reduced by 100 bp, to 39% and 34.5%, respectively.

The improvement in underlying inflation has yet to translate into a
sustained period of low inflation, justifying the CBRT’s preference for a
measured pace of monetary easing.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
President Lula da Silva was re-elected on Sunday for a fourth term
(with 51.8% of the vote), defeating far right-wing candidate Flavio Bol-
sonaro (48.2%). Lula will enter this new term facing significant econo -
mic and political challenges. Public debt has risen to more than 82% of

GDP , while high borrowing costs and rigid mandatory spending leave
limited room to reconcile fiscal consolidation with social spending and
investment. Restoring confidence in the fiscal outlook will be a key
challenge in the short term.
COMMODITIES
Pressure rising. Amid growing rumours of imminent re-escalation of
military operations in the Gulf, energy prices rose sharply this week,
with brent touching $117/b – near its April peak, and European gas

prices (TTF) rising to 97 €/MWh on Monday, their highest level since
Dec 2022. The Asian gas benchmark (JKM) is following the same trend
(4.6% w/w to 31.4 $/mbtu, equivalent to around 96 €/MWh). Diesel
crack spreads are slightly decreasing in Europe (-2.8% w/w on Monday)
and in the US (-4% w/w on Monday). The first Russian diesel tanker
intended to the US market left Russia this Friday and should supply the
US market in 2 weeks. US diesel average retail prices remain oriented

to the upside (0.7% w/w at 6.32 $/gallon on Monday). US commercial
crude inventories fell by 3.1 mb in the week ending 16 October, signi -
ficantly exceeding market expectations for a 340,000-barrel decline.

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United  States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3  according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever -volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2 -0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues . As anticipated by our  Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2).  
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%).  According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2),  driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export gr owth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports ( -1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q).  Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, gr owth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026 , 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate ( AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheles s 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI -related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).
